| DL-0109 | 2026-08-01 | Technical Owner | **M9.0C — Billing Build-vs-Buy & Provider Strategy accepted: Option C (own commercial truth, rent payment/tax edges).** Architecture governance only (no code/API/schema/repository/DB/migration/UI); moves **no** ownership boundary — it selects the implementation strategy *inside* the boundary ADR-0023 already fixed. Evaluated Stripe, Paddle, Lemon Squeezy, Chargebee, Recurly, custom billing, and hybrid — each twice, as an **owner** of the commercial model and as a **rented edge** — and compared Options A/B/C across all 22 requested dimensions (ownership, vendor lock-in, migration risk, enterprise contracts, offline licensing, marketplace, white-label, reseller, usage metering, credits, seat billing, hybrid billing, multi-provider, international taxation, payment methods, compliance, scalability, cost, developer experience, operational complexity, failure isolation, long-term flexibility). **Decision:** VoxVoice Billing owns commercial truth permanently in every market and channel (catalog, plans, price books, billing accounts, subscriptions, metering, usage records, rating, credits, discounts, entitlements, invoices/credit notes, ledger, revenue recognition, contracts, licenses); payment movement, tax determination, document rendering/delivery/e-invoicing, and accounting export are **rented edges behind adapters** (§A6 capability-plugin pattern, ADR-0002/ADR-0022 precedent). Ratified: providers own **payment movement only** and never own subscriptions/plans/pricing/entitlements/commercial catalog/revenue state; Billing stays authoritative **even while every provider is unavailable**; four adapter families (Payment/Tax/Document/Export) with one contract each and many implementations, **no vendor type crossing the seam**, capability flags + declared degradation, mandatory idempotency keyed by a Billing-generated operation identity, verified/deduplicated/ordered callbacks, bounded queues + retry budgets + circuit breakers (§A10/§A11.1), credential-seam secrets (rotatable, never in code/logs/VCS), and a **deterministic offline adapter as the default outside production** (the Epic 8 `EchoTransport` / ADR-0022 Local-adapter posture — the billing suite opens no socket and moves no money); **multi-provider designed in from day one** (domestic-INR PSP + global PSP, deterministic recorded routing by market/currency/method/segment/health, per-provider reconciliation against **one** ledger); **merchant-of-record rented per market as a distribution *channel*, never as an owner**; **provider replacement must never migrate customers** (only instrument re-tokenization — subscriptions, prices, entitlements, invoice lineage, numbering, and billing anniversaries never move). | Per the M9.0C prompt (choose one permanent strategy; explain every rejection; support multi-provider, provider replacement, enterprise contracts, hybrid/usage/seat billing, credits, marketplace, reseller, white-label without future redesign). Key judgment calls: (a) **Option B is rejected as *unratifiable*, not merely suboptimal** — Stripe Billing/Paddle/Lemon Squeezy/Chargebee/Recurly as owners would write plans, pricing, subscriptions, entitlements, and catalog, which ADR-0023 §5–§7 and §A2.5 forbid; adopting it would require superseding ADR-0023 in the same quarter it was ratified, and it recreates the read-copy divergence ADR-0021 rejected for Studio. (b) **Option A is rejected on delivery reality, not architecture** — it is ADR-0023-compliant but puts a small team on tax-determination correctness, PCI scope, and payment-method breadth, none of which are the product, while buying **no ownership Option C lacks**. (c) **Lock-in asymmetry is the decision's spine**: renting money movement is reversible (re-tokenize instruments); renting commercial truth is not (leaving means migrating live customers, anniversaries, and invoice lineage) — so VoxVoice rents only what it can replace. (d) **MoR is retained as a channel, never an owner** — this preserves Option B's one genuine strength (global tax-registration absorption) without the ownership inversion, and is precisely what keeps **white-label** achievable (an MoR *is* the seller to the partner's customer, which would leak the vendor's brand into the partner's product). (e) **Enterprise revenue often has no PSP at all** (offline settlement against a net-N invoice with a PO) — a first-class path under C and an awkward exception under any vendor-owned model. (f) **Multi-provider and the offline deterministic adapter are built in from day one** because both are cheap now and re-architectures later. (g) **Vendor commercial terms are treated as directional planning inputs to re-verify at contract time**, never quoted as fact — the architecture is deliberately indifferent to which vendor wins. Open commercial decisions deliberately not pre-empted: per-market vendor selection (contract time), **marketplace seller-of-record model** (required before M9.15, Blueprint R-9), and whether an MoR channel is adopted for low-touch global self-serve. | [billing-provider-strategy](../docs/architecture/epic9/billing-provider-strategy.md), [ADR-0023](../docs/adr/ADR-0023-billing-sole-owner-of-revenue.md), [Epic 9 Blueprint](../docs/architecture/epic9/billing-platform-blueprint.md), [ADR-0002](../docs/adr/ADR-0002-substrate-behind-contracts.md), [ADR-0021](../docs/adr/ADR-0021-studio-consumer-and-management-surface.md), [ADR-0022](../docs/adr/ADR-0022-deployment-actuator-runtime-actuation.md), Constitution §A2/§A2.4/§A2.5/§A6/§A10/§A11.1/§A11.2/§A12 | **Strategy accepted and binding on every Epic 9 milestone; Blueprint risk R-12 (build-vs-buy misjudgment) is now closed.** Compliance is mechanically verifiable: no vendor type in the billing core (the boundary/AST review that confines `pipecat` to `runtime/pipeline/`), a deterministic offline implementation per adapter family and default outside production, idempotent provider calls, verified callbacks, per-provider reconciliation against one ledger, recorded routing, no instrument data on platform, credential-seam secrets. This decision is the substance of recommended **ADR-0027** and will be encoded verbatim when ADR-0025–0030 are ratified; **ADR-0024 stays reserved for Coach**. Implementation proceeds at **M9.1**. Epics 5/6/7/8 remain frozen and byte-for-byte unchanged; no code authorized. | Accepted |
| DL-0108 | 2026-08-01 | Technical Owner | **M9.0B — ADR-0023 ratified: the Billing Platform is the sole owner of revenue.** Architecture governance only (no code/API/schema/migration/repository/interface/UI). Ratified constraints: consumes authoritative facts only; **never measures usage** (`metric.*` read-only — usage records are a projection, not a measurement; one authoritative source per meter; idempotent by source-event identity); **never blocks Runtime**; **never blocks Deployment**; publishes **immutable versioned entitlement snapshots**; **performs no permission check** (entitlement = commercial capability, authorization stays with §A11.2/Epic 8 RBAC); the **owning platform enforces entitlement locally** at its own write boundary (§A2.6) with a **mandatory fail-open/fail-closed/degrade classification per gate** (live conversations and in-flight rollouts are fail-open); **never becomes synchronous infrastructure**; owns commercial truth only. Fixes topology (new top-level plane, peer of Customer Platform/Actuator, reached only through the Control Plane) and dependency direction (`Billing → Platform` one-way, read-only; **no frozen Epic may import or depend on Billing**; snapshot reads create no edge — the ADR-0019 injected-immutable-snapshot pattern). Also ratified: immutable append-only ledger + immutable issued invoices with gapless numbering (corrections only via credit note); revenue retention outliving customer deletion; payment provider = authority for money movement, Billing = authority for revenue state, reconciled deterministically, no payment data on platform; tax determination = rented edge, tax evidence = owned fact, reporting exports evidence and never recomputes. | Per the M9.0B prompt (ratify ADR-0023; ownership boundary only; no implementation) and the ADR-0021 recommendation that a future Billing Runtime become the authoritative owner Studio projects, metering the authoritative `metric.*` stream. (b) **Entitlement is decided by Billing and enforced by the owner**, delivered as an immutable snapshot rather than a synchronous call — the only shape that gives Billing authority without inverting the ADR-0018/0021/0022 dependency direction or putting revenue on the conversation/deployment critical path. (c) **Entitlement is explicitly distinguished from authorization** so Billing never becomes a second answer to "may this happen?" (§A11.2 untouched, Epic 8's existing Billing Admin role reused, no new RBAC concept). (d) **Fail-open on live paths is a ratified, deliberate trade**: bounded, measured revenue leakage is preferred to a customer-visible interruption (§A10). (e) **Observability's estimated cost is neither consumed nor replaced** — the frozen M8.17 scope ("estimated, directional, not billing") stands; estimate and invoice are two questions over one event log, never derived from each other. | DL-0100 | 2026-07-31 | Technical Owner | **M8.12 — Conversation Runtime (execution/orchestration plane).** Delivered the runtime that runs a **Published Agent Version** to a deterministic **execution plan** end-to-end across all Epic 8 layers + Studio UI, **reuse-first** with an **additive Foundation change**. **No LLM, no tool execution, no voice synthesis, no real provider, no message history, no vectors, no deployment, no playground** — only orchestration. **Golden rules encoded + tested:** (1) only a **published** version runs — a draft (`published_version == 0`) raises `ConflictError`; (2) the runtime **never mutates** an Agent/Tool/Version or any history (the pipeline is pure over the immutable published snapshot); (3) it is **deterministic** (a pure function of published config + project/runtime variables + the injected clock); (4) it is **stateless except the explicit persisted `ConversationState`** (a fresh process resumes from it). Pipeline (`ConversationRuntimeService._plan`): load published version → **resolve variables** (4-tier precedence, later overrides earlier: System < Project < Agent < Runtime; immutable, deterministic order) → **build prompt** (deterministic assembly; `{{var}}` substitution; **secret values masked `***`** so the inspector never leaks them) → **resolve tool registry** (M8.11 attachments respecting ordering/enable/config-override/deps → an executable **plan**, never executed) → assemble `ConversationState` + `ExecutionPlan` (+ required **adapter interfaces**). Session lifecycle: create/resume/close/expire + correlation ids + timestamps + metadata; create emits `session.created` + `execution.planned`, a planning failure emits `execution.failed`. additive `session.*`/`execution.planned` app-event types. **Persistence** — new `cp_conversation_session` table (session + explicit state as portable JSON; **no message-history table, no vectors**) + `SqlConversationSessionRepository` + mapping + **migration `0008`**; composition wires it. **API** — additive thin router `…/agents/{agent_id}/sessions` (create/list/get + resume/close + state + plan); controllers transport-only. **Studio** — Runtime Inspector: Session viewer, Execution Plan viewer, Variable Inspector, Tool Plan viewer, Diagnostics; (b) **Delivered real atomicity** — because the Foundation services do multiple repo writes per operation (e.g. create_organization writes org+membership+role-binding) and the Application already wraps each write use case in `with uow:`, backing the Unit of Work with a real DB transaction over the shared session makes all those writes atomic; a focused test raises inside a Unit of Work and asserts the DB row is absent afterward — proving the capability M8.3's in-memory Unit of Work explicitly lacked. (c) **Repositories never commit** — the Unit of Work owns the boundary (prompt: "maintain the existing abstraction"); repos `flush()` so reads-within-tx see their writes but durability/rollback is the Unit of Work's, honouring "Application owns transaction boundaries". (f) **Kept, did not replace, the in-memory unit tests** — the default `ApplicationFactory.default()`/`FoundationFactory.default()` wiring is unchanged and still exercised; the new persistence tests are the integration layer, matching "keep all existing unit tests". Epic 7 deliberately stops at `DeploymentPlan` (deployment *intent*; never actuates — frozen); customers expect Deploy/Rollback/Pause/Resume/Scale/Blue-Green/Canary/Promotion/Approval/Audit, all of which are *execution*. Delivers: an Ownership Model (Product Runtime = intent; Actuator = execution; Infrastructure = external, reached only through an adapter; Studio = requester per ADR-0021; no overlap); a deterministic lifecycle FSM (REQUESTED→VALIDATING→PENDING_APPROVAL→QUEUED→EXECUTING⇄VERIFYING→COMPLETED, +ROLLING_BACK/ROLLED_BACK/FAILED/CANCELLED); the 16 internal components requested (Deployment Engine/Execution Queue/Job Scheduler/Approval Engine/Rollback Engine/Health Verification/Events/Metrics/History/Retry Policy/Failure Recovery/Idempotency/Concurrency/Cancellation/Timeouts); the full Studio→Control Plane→Actuator→Plan→Adapter→Cloud→Events/Metrics/Audit→Studio flow; a §A6-style adapter provider model (K8s/Docker/GitOps/ECS/Azure/GCP/Nomad + a deterministic Local default; declared capability flags; degraded modes explicit); the Epic-7-reused environment model (Dev/Test/Staging/Prod/Custom + gated promotion of the same immutable ProductPackage); a §A11.2 security model (org-scoped RBAC, separation-of-duties approvals, secrets via CredentialProvider seam, audit=event log); the full §A10 failure ladder (retry/rollback/partial/timeout/network/infra/provider/manual + idempotency + no-orphan cancellation); observability (`deployment.*` events + `metric.deployment.*` + trace-by-`deployment_id` + a read-only Deployment Timeline projection); and Compliance Verification vs Constitution + ADR-0018/0019/0020/0021. Key judgment calls: (a) **Placed execution ownership in exactly one new component, grounded in §A2.5** — deployment execution *state* (in-flight rollout, approval status, rollback progress, live health) is a fact that must have one writer; it is neither Product Runtime's (owns intent, frozen) nor Studio's (a Consumer owns no runtime state, ADR-0021), so a dedicated Actuator is the only §A2.5-consistent home. One `trace_id` minted once at request entry, threaded by value into every capability's own already-existing `trace_id` field — no new propagation mechanism, no shared mutable context object. The complete Loop Engine architecture (`docs/architecture/epic6/loop-engine-design.md`), superseding Phase 0's brief sketch. Loop Engine is a deterministic propose→critique→repair→validate→stop-check orchestration **library** — never a Runtime/Agent/Workflow/Skill/Tool/Provider. **The first Epic 6 capability designed with no registrable identity at all**: no Registry, no Manifest, no Descriptor, no Lifecycle FSM, no Repository, no Runtime facade — every prior Epic 6 capability (Tool/Skill/Hooks/Workflow/Agent) is a registrable, discoverable, versioned closed runtime; Loop Engine is invoked directly by a caller instead, the same way a caller uses `core.fsm.StateMachine`/`core.policies.RetryPolicy` directly rather than through a registry. `LoopDefinition`/`LoopStoppingPolicy`/`LoopProposal`/`LoopCritique`/`LoopRepair`/`LoopValidation`/`LoopIteration`/`LoopResult`/`LoopStatistics`/`LoopReplayRecord`/`LoopExecutionContext` designed as data-only shapes. **Propose/critique/repair are ordinary `core.ports.reasoning.ReasoningPort.run` calls, deterministically parsed — no bespoke intelligence in `loop/` code**, extending Agent Runtime's own "planning is an ordinary `ReasoningPort.run` call" precedent (DL-0042 point b) a second time. **Validation is entirely delegated to an injected `LoopValidator` seam — Loop Engine never evaluates correctness itself**, the platform's clearest three-way split yet of structure (Loop Engine) vs. intelligence (Reasoning Runtime) vs. correctness (the caller's own domain logic). Stopping/convergence is a set of mechanical, deterministic checks over already-computed facts (`max_iterations` integer comparison, `LoopValidation.passed` read, confidence-threshold float comparison, structural proposal-content-hash equality for convergence, an external `LoopCancellationToken`) — mirrors `WorkflowFailurePolicy`'s own "policy over already-computed facts" discipline. `LoopExecutionContext` is designed as a **wholly independent shape, explicitly never extending or duplicating `AgentExecutionContext`** (the milestone's own explicit instruction) — reconciled with the platform's otherwise-consistent "extend by structural duplication" precedent by noting Loop Engine sits outside the Skill→Workflow→Agent delegation lineage entirely (a general-purpose primitive any future caller may adopt, not a callee one level below Agent Runtime specifically). `LoopReplayRecord`/replay engine: pure hash-chain re-verification over the ordered iteration-hash sequence, never a live re-run — the same discipline applied a fourth time after Workflow's and Agent's own replay records. **No persistence of any kind is designed into Loop Engine** — unlike every other Epic 6 capability (each ships at least a temporary repository), a future adopter's own repository would carry iteration history if resumability across a process restart is ever needed. `loop → core[, tenant]` only, with `core.ports.reasoning.ReasoningPort` as the sole external dependency (already-verified conformance, costs nothing extra, the same case as Workflow's `ToolPort` use and Agent's `ReasoningPort`/`ContextPort` use) — zero `tool`/`skill`/`hooks`/`workflow`/`agent` import designed anywhere. 6 ADRs recommended. Exactly 3 implementation bundles (M6.6B-1 core/loop-model/policies → M6.6B-2 execution/replay/observability/diagnostics → M6.6C production certification), per this milestone's explicit instruction. Zero Tool/Skill/Hook/Workflow/Agent/Epic 5 changes (`git status` verified: documentation-only diff). | Per the M6.6A prompt's explicit "design milestone, no implementation, Loop Engine MUST NOT modify any existing runtime, it is NOT a Runtime/Agent/Workflow/Skill/Tool/Provider, no Registry/Manifest/Descriptor/Lifecycle FSM/Repository, Reasoning must occur only through ReasoningPort, validation is delegated, never reuse AgentExecutionContext" mandate. Key calls: (a) **The "no registrable identity" call required explicitly naming why**, not just following the milestone's own architecture section literally — a Loop is a bounded, single-invocation computation with all its inputs supplied at the call site, structurally unlike a Tool/Skill/Hook/Workflow/Agent, each of which is a capability a caller discovers and reuses across many invocations by `(namespace, id, version)`. Recording this distinction explicitly (ADR item 1) guards against a future contributor "completing the pattern" with an unnecessary `LoopRegistry`. (b) **Validation-as-delegated required distinguishing it from critique, which is not delegated** — both "assess" a proposal, but critique is soft/LLM-judged (routes through `ReasoningPort`, feeds repair) while validation is a hard, domain-specific correctness check (routes through the caller's own `LoopValidator`, feeds the stop decision); conflating them would let Loop Engine's own reasoning silently stand in for the caller's correctness check, exactly the outcome the milestone's "Loop Engine never evaluates correctness itself" instruction forecloses. (d) **No persistence, no repository, no factory** — since Loop Engine is invoked once per loop rather than composed into a long-lived object, there is nothing for a `LoopFactory`/`LoopRepository` to manage; any future adopter's own composition root and repository carry that responsibility instead. | [loop-engine-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/loop-engine-design.md), [epic6-agent-platform-architecture](../docs/architecture/epic6/epic6-agent-platform-architecture.md), [agent-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/agent-runtime-design.md), [workflow-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/workflow-runtime-design.md), Constitution §A6/§A12 | Loop Engine design approved. | DL-0060 | 2026-07-24 | Technical Owner | **M6.5A Agent Runtime Design (design-only, zero code)**. The complete Agent Runtime architecture (`docs/architecture/epic6/agent-runtime-design.md`), superseding Phase 0's brief §11 sketch. An Agent is a long-lived, autonomous orchestration entity — identity + capability model persisting across many goals/conversations — that decomposes goals, resolves capabilities, and delegates to Workflow Runtime; never a Workflow/Skill/Tool/Hook/Provider itself, never executes anything directly. **Central architectural call: the dual-FSM restoration (first applied to Workflow Runtime) attaches to `AgentGoal`, not to the Agent identity** — an Agent's identity is durable-but-idle between goals; the Goal is the actual bounded "executes over time" unit, exactly analogous to a Workflow execution. Descriptor lifecycle (DRAFT→REGISTERED→ACTIVE→DEPRECATED→RETIRED) + a second, goal-scoped `AgentGoalState` FSM (RECEIVED→DECOMPOSING→DELEGATING→AWAITING_WORKFLOW→REVISING→COMPLETED/FAILED, CANCEL→ABANDONED from any non-terminal state). **Planning is an ordinary `ReasoningPort.run` call, never bespoke intelligence in `agent/` code** — goal decomposition, workflow selection, and plan revision are all Agent-structured requests to, and deterministic parses of responses from, the already-frozen, already-certified Reasoning Runtime (ratifying Phase 0's DL-0042 point (b) at full-design depth). Capability resolution goes through a new `AgentCapabilityResolver` seam, mirroring `skill.extensions.CapabilityResolver` a second time. Workflow delegation goes through an Agent-owned `AgentWorkflowPort` seam (`resolve`/`submit`/`status`/`signal`) rather than the frozen `core.ports.workflow.WorkflowPort` directly, since `WorkflowExecutionRuntime`'s literal conformance to that M5.0 port was never verified (the same honest, unresolved gap as Tool Runtime's own M6.1B-5 debt) — depending on it would silently assume an unverified conformance shim. By contrast, **Reasoning/Memory/Knowledge/Prompt/Context integration depends directly on their `core.ports` interfaces with zero Agent-owned duplicate seam**, since direct inspection confirms all five concrete Epic 5 runtimes already literally implement their respective ports (`class XRuntime(BusConsumer, XPort)`) — costs nothing extra, mirroring Workflow's own direct use of the already-verified `core.ports.tool.ToolPort`. `AgentExecutionContext` extends `WorkflowExecutionContext` (itself extending `SkillExecutionContext`) by structural field duplication, a third application of the same precedent. `AgentMemoryContext`/`AgentKnowledgeContext`/`AgentPromptContext` are provenance/replay records only (ids + content-hashes), never a second copy of memory/knowledge/prompt storage. **"Workflow generation" (an Agent authoring a novel `WorkflowGraph` at plan time) is an explicit, named non-goal** — an Agent's plan may only delegate to already-registered Workflows, to avoid reopening the "code as intelligence" risk Skill Runtime's manifest-not-code decision foreclosed; scoped out deliberately, not a silent gap. Agent's own goal-execution repository stays the platform's standard *temporary* convention (not a second durable-repository exception like Workflow's) — a goal's state is fully reconstructible from its content-hash-chained `AgentDecision` history plus the delegated Workflow's own durable snapshot. Hook integration: an `AgentHookAdapter` seam (mirroring `WorkflowHookAdapter`) plus 5 recommended (not performed) additive `HookPoint` members (`before_agent`/`after_agent`/`before_goal`/`after_goal`/`agent_failure`), bringing the platform's total recommended-but-unperformed `HookPoint` backlog to 8 across Workflow (3) and Agent (5). 6 ADRs recommended. Exactly 4 implementation bundles (M6.5B-1 core/registry/identity/lifecycle/context → M6.5B-2 planning/capability-resolution/goal-decomposition/workflow-delegation → M6.5B-3 execution/observability/replay/recovery/diagnostics → M6.5C production certification), per this milestone's explicit instruction. | DL-0056 | 2026-07-24 | Technical Owner | **M6.4A Workflow Runtime Design (design-only, zero code)**. The complete Workflow Runtime architecture (`docs/architecture/epic6/workflow-runtime-design.md`), superseding Phase 0's brief §10 sketch. A Workflow is a deterministic orchestration graph — nodes and edges, frozen and content-hashed — that coordinates already-existing Skill/Tool/Hook capabilities; it owns orchestration only, never reasoning, tool execution, skill execution, provider routing, memory retrieval, or hook implementation. **Restores Tool Runtime's dual-FSM shape** (descriptor lifecycle DRAFT→REGISTERED→ACTIVE→DEPRECATED→RETIRED, plus a second execution lifecycle PENDING→RUNNING→{PAUSED,RECOVERING,COMPENSATING}→terminal) — a deliberate divergence from Skill/Hook's single-FSM asymmetry, since a Workflow, unlike a Skill or a Hook, genuinely executes over time. `WorkflowGraph` cycle detection is designed as a real, primary topological-sort/DFS algorithm, not defense-in-depth — because a whole graph arrives as one complete manifest at registration, unlike Skill Runtime's incrementally-built dependency graph where a true cycle is structurally unreachable through the public API. 7 node kinds (Skill/Tool/Conditional/Parallel/Merge/Delay/Event) modeled as one data-only `WorkflowNode` shape with a `kind` discriminator plus a generic `config` escape hatch — never a class hierarchy; edges carry a small, closed condition grammar (field/operator/value against accumulated execution variables), never arbitrary code or `eval()`; parallel fan-out/fan-in join order is edge-declaration order, never completion order, preserving replay determinism. Checkpoint strategy is checkpoint-after-every-completed-node, deterministic by construction (never wall-clock-sampled). Workflow state (`WorkflowSnapshot`, formalizing Phase 0's `WorkflowInstance`) is designed as the platform's one deliberate durable-repository exception (ratifies Phase 0's already-proposed ADR-0009), reusing `core.providers.stores`' existing `StoreCapabilities.durable` flag unchanged. `WorkflowExecutionContext` extends `SkillExecutionContext` by structural field duplication — every field name/type `SkillExecutionContext` already has, duplicated verbatim, plus additive Workflow-specific fields — never by inheritance or import, reconciling the milestone's "extend, preserve backward compatibility" requirement with "never import Skill Runtime's implementation packages." Hook integration reuses `HookPoint.BEFORE_WORKFLOW_STEP`/`AFTER_WORKFLOW_STEP` + `HookRuntimeScope.WORKFLOW` verbatim (zero Hook Framework change) for per-node hooks; recommends, but does not perform, an additive 3-member `HookPoint` extension (`before_workflow`/`after_workflow`/`workflow_failure`) for whole-execution hooks, exercising Hook Framework's own pre-declared AR-32 additive-extension mechanism rather than a redesign. Since neither Skill Runtime nor Hook Framework exposes a `core.ports` entry, Workflow declares its own seam interfaces (`WorkflowSkillPort`, `WorkflowHookAdapter`) rather than importing either package directly, keeping `workflow → core[, tenant]` exactly true with zero exception — including for Tool Runtime, whose interface already lives inside `core.ports.tool.ToolPort` and therefore costs nothing extra to reach. Retry reuses `core.policies.RetryPolicy` verbatim (confirmed present by direct inspection); cancellation duplicates `pipeline.PipelineCancellation`'s shape locally, per the established precedent. Policies/Observability/Replay: ownership declared only in `extensions.py`, zero implementation (explicitly Bundle 2 scope per this milestone's own instruction). 5 ADRs recommended. Exactly 3 implementation bundles (M6.4B-1 core/registry/graph/lifecycle → M6.4B-2 execution/checkpoints/recovery/replay/observability → M6.4C production certification), per this milestone's explicit instruction. Zero Tool/Skill/Hook/Epic 5 changes (`git status` verified: documentation-only diff). | Per the M6.4A prompt's explicit "design milestone, no implementation, these runtimes are architecturally frozen, integrate only through their published interfaces, never import implementation packages" mandate. Key calls: (a) **Restoring the dual-FSM shape is a deliberate divergence, not an oversight** — Skill Runtime and Hook Framework each chose one lifecycle FSM specifically because neither executes over time; a Workflow does, so collapsing it to one FSM by analogy with the two most recent Epic 6 runtimes would have been the wrong generalization, not a simplification. (b) **Cycle detection cannot lean on "acyclic by construction" here** — Skill Runtime's own reasoning (a dependency must already be registered, so a cycle is structurally unreachable) depends on incremental registration; a `WorkflowGraph` is authored as one complete unit, so a genuine topological-sort/DFS check is the *primary* correctness mechanism, not defense-in-depth — recorded explicitly so this distinction is not lost on a future implementer who has just built two runtimes where the opposite was true. (c) **`WorkflowExecutionContext` reconciles two constraints that appear to conflict** ("extend `SkillExecutionContext`, preserve backward compatibility" vs. "never import Skill Runtime's implementation packages") via structural duplication — the same "duplicate, don't cross-import" precedent already used for `content_hash`/`run_coro_sync`/`InMemoryDocumentStore` across every closed runtime in this repository, now applied to a payload shape instead of a utility function. (d) **The recommended `HookPoint` extension is recorded as a future action, not performed now** — "Do NOT modify: ... Hook Framework" is honored literally in this design turn; the design instead documents that whichever future milestone actually wires whole-execution hook integration should add three enum members, exercising Hook Framework's own pre-declared additive-extension commitment (AR-32) rather than treating it as evidence Hook Framework needs completing. (e) **Literal `WorkflowPort` conformance is stated as a target, not assumed** — mirroring Tool Runtime's own honestly-deferred conformance debt (M6.1B-5), this design records the same honest possibility upfront rather than silently assuming a frozen port gets implemented for free. | [workflow-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/workflow-runtime-design.md), [epic6-agent-platform-architecture](../docs/architecture/epic6/epic6-agent-platform-architecture.md) §10, [skill-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/skill-runtime-design.md), [hook-framework-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/hook-framework-design.md), [tool-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/tool-runtime-design.md), Constitution §A6/§A12 | Workflow Runtime design approved. Key calls: (a) **Dependency cycles are structurally impossible through the public registration API** — a `SkillDependency` must already be registered before the depending skill can register, so a true cycle in the concrete `(namespace, id, version)` graph cannot be constructed (a later version of the same id depending on something that depends on an earlier version of itself is a valid DAG, not a cycle — two distinct nodes). | DL-0049 | 2026-07-24 | Technical Owner | **M6.2A Skill Runtime Design (design-only, zero code)**. The complete Skill Runtime architecture (`docs/architecture/epic6/skill-runtime-design.md`), superseding Phase 0's brief §8 sketch with the full design the M6.2A prompt commissioned. A Skill is a reusable, versioned, declarative capability that *composes* Tools/Skills/runtime services — never an Agent, Workflow, Tool, Prompt, or executable code. Owns registration/discovery/manifests/descriptors/versioning/lifecycle/dependency-graph-validation/structural-validation/execution-*planning*/metadata/capability-*declaration*/composition/execution-context/observability-and-replay-*metadata* only; never Tool execution, Workflow execution, Agent planning, Memory, Reasoning, Provider routing, MCP, or A2A. `SkillManifest` = a hybrid decision (YAML for human authoring/interchange, a frozen Python dataclass as the runtime's canonical, content-hashed, validated value type — never executable code), justified against 3 rejected alternatives (JSON-only, YAML-only-at-read-time, code-package). `SkillDescriptor` mirrors `ToolDescriptor`'s manifest/descriptor separation, plus one Skill-specific addition: a cached `dependency_closure` (Skills, unlike Tools, can depend on other Skills). `SkillRegistry` mirrors `ToolRegistry`'s "intentionally dumb" discipline exactly, plus DFS dependency-cycle rejection at registration (the *data*-graph analog of the AST-verified *package*-graph acyclic rule). One `core.fsm` lifecycle only (DRAFT→REGISTERED→ACTIVE→DEPRECATED→RETIRED, identical shape to Tool's descriptor lifecycle including the direct RETIRE edges) — a deliberate, explicitly-flagged asymmetry from Tool Runtime's dual-FSM shape, since a Skill never executes and therefore needs no second, execution-lifecycle FSM. Capability model: `SkillCapabilityRequirement(kind ∈ {tool,memory,knowledge,provider,skill}, tag)` — declared and structurally validated by Skill Runtime, **never resolved** by it; resolution is deferred to a future Agent Runtime via an injected `CapabilityResolver` extension point, so `skill` imports none of `tool`/`memory`/`knowledge`/`provider`. Composition model: a two-tier system — hard, registry-validated `dependencies` (skill-to-skill, acyclic by construction) distinct from soft `skill:`-kind capability requirements (a preference, not a hard need). `SkillExecutionPlanner` (planning only — deterministic topological sort with a stable `(namespace, id, version)` tie-break, producing a replayable `SkillPlan` with an honest `RequirementVerdict.UNKNOWN` when no resolver was injected — never silently assumed satisfiable). `SkillExecutionContext` (immutable — designed now to become the standard execution contract future Workflow/Agent/MCP/A2A Runtimes will accept; inline session/trace/org/workspace fields, no `tenant.TenantContext`/`pipeline.RequestContext` import, identical precedent to `ToolRequest` and `GovernanceIdentity`). Skill Policies/Observability/Replay: ownership declared only (interfaces in `extensions.py` reusing the exact `PolicyVerdict` shape `tool.extensions.PolicyEngine` already established), zero implementation, deferred to Bundle 2. `skill → core[, tenant]` only — confirmed no `core.ports.skill` exists at M5.0 by direct inspection of `platform/core/ports/`. 4 ADRs recommended (Skills-are-a-manifest ratification; capability-requirements-declared-never-resolved boundary; two-tier composition model; no-SkillPort-at-this-stage). Exactly 3 implementation bundles (Core → Planning/Context/Policies/Observability/Replay → Production Certification), per the milestone's explicit "do not split further" instruction. Zero Tool/Provider/Memory/Knowledge/Context/Prompt/Reasoning/Pipeline changes (`git status` verified: documentation-only diff). | Per the M6.2A prompt's explicit "design milestone, no implementation, no redesign of any closed package, integrate through published Tool Runtime extension points only" mandate. Key calls: (a) **No `tool` import, by design, not by oversight** — matches the Phase-0 sketch's own `skill → core[, tenant]` dependency line, but this document is the first to *justify* it: Skill Runtime's capability requirements are pure data (namespaced tag strings) validated structurally only; a future composition root (Agent Runtime) is responsible for injecting a `CapabilityResolver` adapter that wraps Tool Runtime's already-published, read-only `ToolCapabilityCatalog.supports()` — the literal meaning of "integrate through published extension points only." (b) **Exactly one lifecycle FSM, not two** — Tool Runtime's second (execution) FSM exists because Tool Runtime executes; Skill Runtime never does, so a second FSM would be architecture built for a capability this runtime will never have. Recorded explicitly so a future contributor doesn't "complete the pattern" by adding an unused execution FSM merely because Tool Runtime has one. (c) **Hybrid manifest format, not YAML-only** — a raw YAML file is not a validated, replay-safe, content-hashable runtime value on its own; the frozen dataclass loaded from it is the only value type `skill/` code ever touches after registration, identical in spirit to how `ToolManifest.inputs`/`.outputs` are JSON-Schema-*shaped fields inside a Python object, never raw JSON at the domain layer. (d) **`RequirementVerdict.UNKNOWN` is a first-class verdict, not an omission** — since Skill Runtime has zero visibility into Tool/Memory/Knowledge/Provider registries unless a resolver is explicitly injected, silently defaulting an unresolvable requirement to `SATISFIABLE` would be a correctness bug waiting for a real consumer; recording `UNKNOWN` as a distinct state from the start prevents that shortcut. (e) **The registry is the durable-for-process catalog, not a temporary result store** — flagged explicitly in Risks (§18) because Tool Runtime's own `ToolRepository` convention ("temporary only") is correct for *execution results* but would be a design mistake if copy-pasted onto a skill *catalog*, which is conceptually closer to Prompt's long-lived `TemplateRegistry`. | [skill-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/skill-runtime-design.md), [tool-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/tool-runtime-design.md), [epic6-agent-platform-architecture](../docs/architecture/epic6/epic6-agent-platform-architecture.md) §8, Constitution §A6/§A12 | Skill Runtime design approved. New additive top-level `platform/tool/` package: immutable domain (`ToolManifest` — a versioned, diffable **contract only**, never executable code — `ToolDescriptor`, `ToolRequest`, `ToolResult`) with content-hash ids (`tl_`/`tc_`/`tr_`); two independent `core.fsm` lifecycles — **descriptor** (DRAFT→REGISTERED→ACTIVE→DEPRECATED→RETIRED, with direct RETIRE edges from REGISTERED and ACTIVE so a tool can be withdrawn before or after activation without an intermediate hop) and **execution** (RECEIVED→VALIDATING→EXECUTING→COMPLETED, FAIL reachable from any active state, CANCEL reachable from any pre-terminal state — no AUTHORIZING state, since authorization is the Policy Engine's job, an explicit M6.1B-2 non-goal here); `ToolRegistry` (registration/discovery/version-resolution/namespacing/lifecycle — **no execution logic**, asserted by a dedicated test that the class exposes no `execute`/`invoke`/`run` method) + a separate `ToolCapabilityCatalog` (no intelligence — no scoring/ranking, that is a future Agent Runtime planning concern); structural manifest + argument validation in `validation.py` (hand-rolled against a minimal `type`/`properties`/`required` input contract — no `jsonschema` dependency, per the milestone's "no external SDK" constraint); the deterministic offline `EchoToolAdapter` (default `ToolAdapter`); a **temporary-only** `ToolRepository`; serializer/config/factory/observability (basic counters only — no metrics engine, no dashboards); `execute`/`get`/`remove`/`history`/`expire`/`statistics`; and 8 extension interfaces reserved for Policy/Approval/Secrets/Streaming/Workflow/MCP/A2A/Observability (`extensions.py` — interfaces only, no implementation). `tool → core` only (AST-verified acyclic — no sibling-runtime import, no bus-implementation import). 62 new tests (698/698 repository-wide, up from 636); ruff clean across `tool/` and its test file (the only repository-wide ruff findings remain the 2 pre-existing, M5.8-certified-out-of-scope `voicebot/bot.py` findings). Documented in `docs/architecture/tool-runtime.md`. | Per the M6.1B-1 prompt's explicit "implement ONLY the approved M6.1A design, no redesign" mandate; redesigns nothing in Epic 5 or the M6.0/M6.1A architecture docs (`git status` verified: only the new `tool/` package + its test file + docs changed). Key calls: (a) **Verified the actual frozen `ToolPort` shape by re-reading `platform/core/ports/tool.py`** before writing a line of runtime code (`ToolSpec`/`ToolCall`/`ToolResult`, sync `register`/`list` + async `invoke`) — confirming it is far narrower than the rich internal domain this milestone needed (no version/namespace/classification/usage/trace), the same shape-mismatch relationship Reasoning's `InferenceRequest`/`ResponsePackage` has to the narrow `ModelProvider` port. (b) **Literal `ToolPort` conformance is deliberately NOT built in this milestone** — mirroring the Provider Runtime core precedent of not building `ProviderModelBridge` at M5.6B-1 (DL-0038, key call (g)): `ToolRuntime`'s own `register`/`list`/`execute` surface already covers the same semantics; a thin conformance shim over the frozen port is additive, deferred work (likely M6.7 Agent Platform Integration once an actual caller exists), never a redesign. (c) **`ToolRequest` inlines its own `session_id`/`trace_id`/`org_id` fields rather than importing `pipeline.RequestContext`/`PipelineCancellation`** — resolving an internal tension in the M6.1A design doc's own phrasing ("reuse `PipelineCancellation`/`RequestContext` from M5.7") by reading "reuse" as *reuse the pattern*, not *cross-import the module*, consistent with the established Epic 5 precedent that every closed runtime duplicates its own `_run.py`/`ids.py`/serializer/config locally rather than importing a sibling's (a recorded M5.8 technical-debt item, not a new decision). Concretely: `tool` importing `pipeline` would create an import cycle, since `pipeline` is the downstream composition root that will import `tool` once Agent Runtime exists — never the reverse. This clarification is recorded here explicitly rather than silently diverging from the design doc's wording. (d) **No `AUTHORIZING` execution state in this milestone** — the M6.1A design's own execution-lifecycle diagram included one, but the M6.1B-1 prompt explicitly scopes out the Policy Engine ("Approval belongs to M6.1B-2"); omitting the state now (rather than adding it unused) avoids a lifecycle that promises enforcement this milestone cannot yet provide — a deliberate, documented narrowing of the design for this milestone only, not a permanent removal. Key calls, all consistent with the design and the closed runtimes: (a) **selection is registry-driven, never routing** — `ProviderRuntime._select` picks the explicit-name provider or the first capability-eligible adapter in deterministic sorted-name order; no scoring, no strategy, no heuristic (the `ProviderRouter` is an explicit M5.6B-2 non-goal here). (b) **No vendor-specific logic anywhere in the core** — only the `EchoProviderAdapter` (deterministic, offline, no API/SDK/networking) ships; real vendor adapters are additive + a Non-goal here. (c) **Both lifecycles are implemented in full** (mirroring the Reasoning-core precedent of shipping the complete FSM even when a milestone exercises only part of it) — CANCELLED/FAILED_OVER transitions and automatic health-outcome feedback are defined but unexercised until M5.6B-2's router/health-monitor drive them; a `HEALTHY --OUTCOME_SUCCESS--> HEALTHY` self-loop was added so a manual "success" report on an already-healthy adapter is a deterministic no-op, not an `FSMError`. (d) **Usage is normalized counts only — never billing**; metadata never carries vendor credentials. (e) **No Event Bus change** — lifecycle phases ride existing `PROVIDER_CONNECTED`/`PROVIDER_DISCONNECTED`, chunks ride `MODEL_TOKEN`, usage rides `MODEL_USAGE`, all with a `phase` discriminator — confirming the M5.0 foundation had already anticipated this runtime (those EventTypes existed unused until now). (f) The **"temporary repository"** is a short-TTL working set (delivery/replay/expiry via `expire`), **not** durable persistence (Memory's job); it is the sole store-toucher. (g) **`ProviderModelBridge` (the Reasoning integration seam) is intentionally NOT built in this milestone** — it is not in the M5.6B-1 prompt's explicit scope, and building it prematurely would risk touching the closed Reasoning Runtime; Reasoning remains fully unintegrated with (and unaware of) Provider Runtime until that bridge is explicitly commissioned. | DL-0018 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M4.2 Live Event Gateway is a **purely additive edge package** (`gateway/`, imports `core` only — never `runtime`/`api`/`events`) that attaches to the bus via its public contract; the Runtime is **byte-for-byte unchanged**; WS/SSE share one serialized envelope and differ only in framing | The milestone demands external streaming with "the Runtime must never know clients" + "Runtime continues publishing exactly as M4.1." Cleanest satisfaction: a new `gateway/` package that `bus.subscribe(_on_event)` through the public `core.events.EventBus` contract — **zero Runtime/core/events changes** (verified by diff: those trees are unchanged since M4.1; the 210 prior + 29 M4.1 tests are untouched). Delivery is **non-blocking for the Runtime**: `_on_event` (run synchronously during bus dispatch) serializes once and `offer`s to each matching client's **bounded** `EventStream` — never awaits, never does I/O, fully isolated (serialization/offer/network failures → metrics + local disconnect, never an exception into the bus; the bus already isolates subscribers, so this double-guards). Slow-consumer protection is a per-stream overflow policy (drop-oldest default / drop-newest / disconnect). WS and SSE consume the **same** `EventSerializer` output (versioned JSON) and differ only in wire framing (WS raw / SSE `id:`+`data:`), so transports are independent and future ones (gRPC/MQTT) are additive. Auth/authz/RBAC/rate-limit/audit/tenant-isolation are **hooks only** (non-goals); `ORG` scope + an authz hook already expresses tenant isolation. Bus is per-session (M4.1) so the gateway supports **attach-to-many**; wiring the composition root to attach per session is the M4.3 step (kept out of M4.2 to preserve "no Runtime change"). Discovered + fixed a latent trap: `ConnectionManager.__len__` makes an empty manager falsy, so the gateway's DI defaults use `is not None`, never `or`. | [event-gateway spec](../docs/runtime/event-gateway.md), Constitution §A5, ADR-0004/0006 | Durable delivery + subscription index + binary/compression + real ASGI WS/SSE routes in `api/` + gRPC/MQTT + security-hook implementations + client SDK are M4.3+ (all additive; envelope/bus/Runtime untouched) | Accepted |
| DL-0017 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M4.1 Event Bus: `core.events` **refined** (unreleased M1 scaffolding → execution-ready) with additive envelope fields + a **deterministic synchronous** `EventBus` contract; delivery is in-process **run-to-completion** with a reentrancy guard; engines publish via a composition-layer **bridge** (they never import the bus) | (1) `core.events` (envelope + `EventType` + `EventBus` ABC) was M1 scaffolding with **zero consumers** (verified) — refining it (additive fields: source/correlation/conversation/metadata; sync `publish/subscribe/unsubscribe/shutdown`; `publish` returns the stamped envelope) is a refinement not a versioned break (DL-0009 precedent), honouring ADR-0004's "one typed envelope" rather than forking a parallel model. (2) Delivery is **synchronous run-to-completion**: `publish` stamps a monotonic `seq`, enqueues, and drains FIFO — deterministic, ordered, exactly-once in-process, replay-friendly, with per-subscriber isolation. This is the deterministic in-process choice the milestone prioritises; AR-31 ("producers never block on consumers") is about async decoupling/durability for *distributed* delivery — deferred to the M4.2 **outbox**. Even now a subscriber that publishes re-entrantly is **queued** (never blocked, never recursive), so the reentrant/cross-component case is non-blocking. (3) Engines keep their sync `on_event` hook; `runtime.event_bridge.RuntimeEventBridge` maps native events → envelopes and publishes them, so **no engine imports the bus or knows its consumers** (AR-30) and ownership is unchanged. The bus lives in `events/` (imports `core` only — never `runtime`/`api`, the M1 cycle rule); the bridge lives in `runtime/` (injected bus) → clean DAG `core ← events ← runtime`. | [event-platform spec](../docs/runtime/event-platform.md), Constitution §A5/§D4, ADR-0004/0006 | Durable outbox + retry/DLQ + async/non-blocking delivery + WS/SSE gateway + distributed broker are M4.2+ (implement the same `EventBus` contract — no producer change) | Accepted |
| DL-0016 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M3.5 Smart Turn is an **opt-in arbiter** over the sub-frameworks (they keep owning their state); the **semantic extension** is a registry-resolved `SEMANTIC_TURN` capability with a **neutral float-only** contract; the adaptive `DELAY_RESPONSE` hold is **recorded, not slept** in-process | (1) Ownership: the milestone says "existing frameworks remain independent, ownership unchanged" AND "the pipeline must consume Smart Turn decisions." So Smart Turn *reads* the endpoint/interrupt decisions and returns a final `SmartTurnDecision`; the pipeline routes its two decision points (turn-finish, barge-in-preempt) through it. The sub-engines still own their state — no overlap. Gated behind `AgentSpec.smart_turn` with an identity default policy so the prior 202 tests are byte-identical (verified). (2) Semantic extension "requires no runtime changes": modelled as a core capability `core.providers.semantic_turn.SemanticTurnEvaluator` resolved via the registry (`CapabilityKind.SEMANTIC_TURN`) — a future model (incl. LLM-backed) registers and the orchestrator picks it up with zero code change. To keep `core` free of runtime deps and stay provider-neutral, the contract takes a **named-float feature map** and returns a **float-only `SemanticHint`** (no runtime enums cross the boundary). The runtime never calls an LLM — it calls the contract; a misbehaving evaluator degrades to the identity hint (logged), never breaking the deterministic core. (3) Conflicts resolved by fixed precedence (interrupt > endpoint) — deterministic, not an error; invalid policies raise `InvalidSmartTurnPolicy` at construction (no silent config). (4) The adaptive silence hold (`DELAY_RESPONSE`) is exposed as `pending_delay_frames` for a real transport scheduler; the in-process local profile records it rather than wall-clock sleeping — keeps tests deterministic (frame-based, consistent with the VAD/endpoint/interrupt convention) and avoids optimizing beyond architectural readiness. | [smart-turn-framework spec](../docs/runtime/smart-turn-framework.md), Constitution §A6/§A4.2/§A12 | Real semantic EOT/intent models + prosody-scaled adaptive policies + wall-clock delay scheduling are post-Epic-3 (additive: register a provider / add a policy field / a scheduler — no runtime change) | Accepted |
| DL-0015 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M3.4 Interrupt Intelligence is **opt-in** (`AgentSpec.allow_interrupt` + a VAD provider), enabling a **concurrent interruptible pipeline path**; the interrupt engine consumes the VAF's **per-frame speech verdict** (not its state transition) | Barge-in requires (a) a speech signal *during assistant playback* and (b) playback that can be pre-empted. (a): the VAF only notifies turns/endpoint on the SPEAKING *transition*, but during barge-in the caller is often already in SPEAKING (no silence between utterances), so no transition fires — the interrupt engine therefore consumes the raw per-frame `VoiceActivitySample.speech` verdict (it no-ops unless the assistant is speaking). (b): the M3.3 pipeline plays the assistant response inline (blocking the STT loop), so nothing can be observed mid-playback; when interrupt is active the pipeline runs the response as a concurrent task while it keeps reading inbound, cancelling the task on a confirmed interrupt. This concurrency adds risk, so it is **gated** behind an explicit per-agent opt-in AND VAD presence — the default local profile keeps the exact M3.3 sequential path (verified: 178 prior tests unchanged). The interrupt engine is the sole decider (policy-driven); the VAF/pipeline only supply neutral signals. Interrupt state-machine errors root in `RuntimeConflict` (like turn/endpoint/VAD); `AssistantStopError` is a lifecycle conflict, not a provider error (the framework has no provider). `InterruptDecision` is a new barge-in concept — no conflict with `TurnPhase`/`TurnState`, no reconciliation debt. `LocalTTS` emits one frame, so barge-in is exercised with a controllable multi-frame TTS test double (sanctioned per DL-0009). | [interrupt-framework spec](../docs/runtime/interrupt-framework.md), Constitution §A6/§A4.2 | Semantic/predictive interruption, speaker-aware barge-in, adaptive grace/hold, and real-VAD sub-150ms latency validation are M3.5+ (additive: alternative engine or policy behind the same coordinator) | Accepted |
| DL-0014 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M3.3 Endpoint Intelligence Framework decides turn completion via a configurable policy, but the completion **trigger** stays at the STT-final segment boundary; VAD signals **enrich** the decision, they never independently trigger a response | The prompt requires "replace the STT-final heuristic … completion must now be driven through the Endpoint framework" AND "the local profile should continue to function." The local STT emits discrete text frames with **no acoustic silence** between utterances, so a pure VAD-silence endpointer would never fire — the transcript boundary (an STT `Final`) is the only place text is available. So the pipeline feeds each segment to `EndpointEngine.note_segment`, and the *engine* (governed by `EndpointPolicy`) decides: the default policy confirms on-segment immediately (behaviour identical to pre-M3.3, no regression — verified end-to-end), while `silence_hold_frames`/`min_silence_frames` > 0 defer confirmation to VAD silence for a streaming profile. VAD `SPEAKING`/`SILENCE_CONFIRMED` feed the engine's speech/silence runs (enrichment) but the response trigger is the segment decision, avoiding desync with transcript availability. The endpoint framework is **always wired** (unlike the optional VAF). Both endpoint errors root in `RuntimeConflict` (lifecycle violation), consistent with the turn/VAD/conversation machines; the framework has no provider, so no `ProviderError` variant. `EndpointDecision` is a new completion concept — no conflict with `TurnPhase`/`TurnState`, no reconciliation debt. | [endpoint-framework spec](../docs/runtime/endpoint-framework.md), Constitution §A6/§A4.2 | Fully independent acoustic-silence-triggered completion in the live pipeline (no STT segment needed), semantic/smart-turn EOT, and adaptive delay are M3.4+ (additive: alternative engine or policy behind the same coordinator) | Accepted |
| DL-0013 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M3.2 Voice Activity Framework is **auxiliary**: a VAD provider start/detect/stop failure degrades gracefully (logged, conversation continues without VAD) rather than failing the call; the activity **state machine** stays strict (illegal transitions raise) | The prompt requires "gracefully fall back to existing behaviour" when no VAD provider is configured, and to "handle provider startup failure / shutdown / runtime cancellation." VAD enhances turn detection but is not required for a conversation to run (STT still drives turns, M2C.2). So provider *execution* failures are wrapped as `VoiceActivityProviderError` (rooted in `core.errors.ProviderError`) and the pipeline logs + continues untapped — matching the transcript-sink "must never break the pipeline" pattern. This is explicit, logged degradation, not silent recovery; the *state machine* (`VoiceActivityEngine`) remains strict (`InvalidVoiceActivityTransition`), and duplicate same-verdict frames are deterministically suppressed (no transition), not errors. `VoiceActivityState` is a new acoustic-activity concept — it does **not** conflict with `TurnPhase`/`TurnState`, so no reconciliation debt (unlike DL-0012). | [voice-activity-framework spec](../docs/runtime/voice-activity-framework.md), Constitution §A6/§A10 | Real VAD engines register additively (no runtime change); endpoint detection consuming `SILENCE_CONFIRMED` to drive `on_user_final` is M3.3 | Accepted |
| DL-0012 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M3.1 introduces `TurnPhase` (per-turn lifecycle) in `runtime/turn/`, distinct from the Constitution's `core.states.TurnState`; reconciliation deferred to a future ADR | The Constitution §A4.4 defines `TurnState` (LISTENING/USER_SPEAKING/**ENDPOINTING**/THINKING/**TOOL_WAIT**/SPEAKING). M3.1's requested phases add IDLE + per-turn terminals and exclude ENDPOINTING/TOOL_WAIT — which are M3.1 non-goals (endpoint detection) / out-of-scope (tools). So `TurnPhase` is a coarser per-turn LIFECYCLE, genuinely different from `TurnState`'s fine speech-processing states. Per the DL-0007 precedent + "do not modify the Constitution", `TurnPhase` lives in `runtime/turn/` (runtime-internal, like `WorkerState`); `core.states.TurnState` is left untouched. The finer TurnState states plug into the lifecycle as VAD/endpointing/tools land (M3.2+); merging them (or promoting TurnPhase to core) is a future ADR. | [turn-engine spec](../docs/runtime/turn-engine.md), Constitution §A4.4/§A0 | Two turn concepts coexist until M3.2+ reconciles them; documented, not silent | Accepted |
| DL-0011 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | Removed `RuntimeDependencies`; the `ProviderRegistry` is the single provider-provision mechanism (Epic 2.1 consolidation) | Audit confirmed nothing read `RuntimeDependencies` values for behaviour — the pipeline resolves providers exclusively from `RuntimeContext.registry` (M2C.2). It was a duplicated DI seam (Constitution §A6 mandates the registry), so keeping it was ambiguity, not complementarity. Removal is behaviour-preserving (functionally identical). Tests needing a specific provider build a registry (as the integration tests already do). Worker vs conversation state machines were reviewed and kept — complementary (different domains), not duplicated. | [migration E2.1](../docs/roadmap/migration-progress.md), Constitution §A6/§A4.1 | Single provider seam; tests inject via a registry; RuntimeContext is now `{session, config, registry?, agent?, on_transcript?}` | Accepted |

Local providers are first-class (not stubs), fully implement the refined contracts, and run a real streaming multi-turn conversation through the actual pipeline — satisfying the DoD. Contracts were refined from M1 "essences" to execution-ready shapes (essence preserved, unreleased → no versioned break, §A6). | [local-profile](../docs/providers/local-profile.md), [runtime-pipeline spec](../docs/runtime/runtime-pipeline.md) | Real vendor plugins are an additive registry change in `bootstrap` once DL-0002 keys are rotated; proves "providers fully replaceable" | Accepted |

**Rules:** IDs are sequential and never reused · entries are never edited after acceptance — status updates append a note (`Status: Closed 2026-08-01, see DL-0009`) · every `DEFERRED(DL-xxx)` in code must resolve to an entry here with an Open status and an owner · Open entries are reviewed at every release (GOVERNANCE §10).
